### Task P (conditional): M3.1 E7/R7 miss

**Run only if** M3.1 recorded an E7 performance miss or an R7 seek-latency miss that M3.1 did not fix
(its entries "Task 11b: perf" and, if Task C ran, "Task C" in the M3 plan file's `## Changes during
execution`, and spec §16 "Playback throughput (M3 `perf`)"); Task 0 of M4 reads them and names the
miss. Otherwise skip: nothing is built. When it runs, it runs after Task 7b and before Task 9
(one task at a time per repository: it may change dvv-media, dvv-gpu or rawreel, which Tasks 2–7b
change or build); Task 9 waits for it (U-M4ORDER: Task 9 now follows Task 7b, and Task 8 comes
after Task 10-final).

**Goal:** Take the next fallback that M3's risk table names for the recorded bottleneck, build only
that one, re-measure with `rawreel-gui --selftest perf`, and record the outcome.

**Files, interfaces and behaviour by the recorded miss** (do only the branch that matches; Step 1
names it and the controller confirms it before any code):
- **Throughput miss (E7: `actualFps < 59.5` or `stalls > 0`), M3 R3 order — the first item not
  already done that addresses the named stage:**
  1. **ToDisplay from the original buffer (M3 F4), when the stage is unpack or ToDisplay:**
     dvv-media: a fused converter step that reads the packed original and writes the display image
     in one dispatch (the unpack shader code as a Slang module used by both shaders), chosen by the
     planner instead of `GpuUnpack` + `ToDisplay` when nothing else needs the planes. Tests: the
     M3 ToDisplay matrix (37 formats × 8 colour sets) through the fused step equals the two-step
     path exactly; the converter stats show the fused step; sync validation zero.
  2. **Two frames in flight in the presenter (M1b F1), when the stage is present/acquire wait:**
     dvv-gpu: a per-frame-slot fence and command buffer pair (2 slots), the provider called for
     slot `n % 2` while the other may be in flight; `PresenterLogic` cases for two outstanding
     frames; the M1b presenter tests (resize, minimise, device lost) unchanged and green ×10 in
     Debug (the Debug race check of Step 3, regex `dvv_gpu\.present`) and ×10 under `vs2026-asan`
     at the Task 9 checkpoint (U-WIN: no TSan on Windows; the threading change gets a second
     reviewer's race review instead; U-PROC).
  3. **Mailbox (M1b F2), when `overwritten` or vsync waits dominate:** dvv-gpu
     `PresenterOptions` gains a present-mode preference (`fifo` default, `mailbox` when supported);
     rawreel-gui uses `mailbox` for `--selftest perf` only if the measurement shows a gain, else
     nothing changes. Tests: mode selection falls back to FIFO when MAILBOX is not offered; the
     present tests pass with both.
- **Overwritten miss only (`overwritten > 6` with fps met), M3 R13:** lock the media clock to the
  present cadence: rawreel `PlayerLogic` takes the presenter's measured refresh period when it is
  within 0.5 % of the stream's frame period and schedules shows on present ticks. Tests: a
  `PlayerLogic` case with a 59.94 Hz cadence and 60 fps content shows every index once, in order.
- **Seek miss (R7, `seekMaxMs > 250`) after M3.1 Task C ran:** M3 names no further fallback: record
  the numbers and send the miss to the arbiter; build nothing without a new decision. If M3.1's Task
  C did not run although triggered, do M3.1 plan Task C as written there (its text is binding).

Every branch keeps the public behaviour of the other layers; new public API is limited to what the
branch names.

**Tests first:** the branch's cases above fail first (missing step, field or behaviour).

**Steps** (from the workspace root; PowerShell; Linux (clang-format only) through `wsl … env.sh`;
git only in PowerShell, except the read-only `git diff --name-only` inside the WSL format command;
nothing is pushed). Record `<base>` (`git -C <repo> rev-parse HEAD`) before Step 2. Task P falls
under the Task 9 checkpoint (Release, ASan with its race check ×10, clang-tidy and the Linux build
of any Linux-only code it changes):
- [ ] **Step 1: Read the M3.1 records**
  (`rawreel-workspace/docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-23-m3-player.md` (named exactly: `*m3*` also
  matches the M3.1 plan),
  sections "Task 11b: perf" and "Task C"; spec §16). State the miss, the named stage and the branch
  in a short report to the controller; wait for the confirmation.
- [ ] **Step 2: Tests first, implementation, commit** `perf: <what>` in the changed repository
  (here-string to `git -C <repo> commit -F -`, trailer
  `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`; on the repository's `m4` branch,
  which Task 0 created).
- [ ] **Step 3: Per-task checks on that final commit:** clang-format on the changed files
  `wsl -d Ubuntu-24.04 -e bash -lc '. _build/tools/linux/env.sh && cd <repo> && git diff --name-only -z --diff-filter=d <base> -- "*.h" "*.cpp" | xargs -0 -r clang-format --dry-run --Werror && echo FORMAT-OK'`;
  Windows Debug (one background call)
  `$env:DVV_REQUIRE_GPU='1'; $env:DVV_VK_VALIDATION='sync'; $env:DVV_VK_VALIDATION_REQUIRED='1'; $env:DVV_REQUIRE_FFMPEG='1'; Push-Location <repo>; cmake --preset vs2026 | Out-Null; cmake --build --preset vs2026; ctest --preset vs2026-small --output-on-failure 2>&1 | Select-String 'tests passed|tests failed|Skipped|error'; Pop-Location`;
  then the same call for `rawreel` (it consumes the change); for the presenter branch (2), the
  Debug race check
  `$env:DVV_REQUIRE_GPU='1'; $env:DVV_VK_VALIDATION='sync'; $env:DVV_VK_VALIDATION_REQUIRED='1'; Push-Location dvv-gpu; ctest --preset vs2026 -R "dvv_gpu\.present" --repeat until-fail:10 --output-on-failure 2>&1 | Select-Object -Last 5; Pop-Location`;
  a Linux-only code change (an Xlib/posix path of the presenter, for example) is compiled by the
  Task 9 checkpoint build; the privacy lint
  `cmake -DREPO=<repo> -DMODE=commits "-DEXTRA_TERMS=$env:USERNAME;$env:COMPUTERNAME" -P rawreel-workspace/tools/privacy-lint.cmake`.
- [ ] **Step 4: Re-measure** with M3.1 plan Task 11b Steps 5–7 (build Release first, build only:
  `Push-Location rawreel; cmake --build --preset vs2026-release; Pop-Location`; the 4K
  `yuv420p10le` file in `$env:TEMP/rawreel-perf`, Release, validation off, three runs, the median by `actualFps`, the E7
  rule; the seek numbers for an R7 branch).
- [ ] **Step 5: Record** "Task P: <branch>" (before/after numbers, the verdict) in the M4 plan
  file's `## Changes during execution`; update the spec §16 item "Playback throughput (M3 `perf`)"
  with the new numbers; commit `docs: record the playback performance after <branch>`
  (rawreel-workspace, with the trailer); privacy lint on rawreel-workspace. Delete the perf folder.

**Expected results:** the branch's tests green in vs2026 Debug (GPU tests run on NVIDIA, sync
validation zero; the presenter branch ×10 in Debug); `FORMAT-OK`; the new perf numbers recorded
with the E7 verdict. (Release, `vs2026-asan` with the ×10, clang-tidy and the Linux build: Task 9
checkpoint.) A remaining miss
goes to the arbiter with the bottleneck named; it does not block the later tasks (9, 10-alpha to
10-final, 8, 11, 12) unless the arbiter says so.

**Commit:** `perf: <what>` in the changed repository; `docs: record the playback performance after
<branch>` (rawreel-workspace).

**Notes:**
- Design references: Y9, design v2 §10 Task P ("as v1"), v1 Task P (fallback order of M3 R3/R7);
  M3 R3, R7, R13, F4, X4; M3.1 Task C; M1b F1, F2.
- **Note (plan choice):** only the first fallback of the matching branch is built; further items
  need a new measurement and a controller decision (as M3.1 Task B).
- **Note (plan choice):** the controller confirms the branch before code, because the branch
  follows from a measured stage that the plan cannot know.

The table above records the plan v2 arbiter fixes as applied then. Where it names a Linux step or a
step number that U-WIN removed or moved (rows 1, 7, 8, 21, 26, 27), the task texts and the "U-WIN

## U-WIN changes

User decision U-WIN (`decisions.md`, 2026-10-23, binding): v1.0 supports Windows only; the code
stays portable; no Linux tests; one Linux sanity build only in the final verification task and in
tasks that change Linux-only code or build files. Applied to this plan before execution (the plan
had not started). Deferred items are in `future.md` as "[M4->v1.x] … (reason: U-WIN)".

**Task mapping:** unchanged. No task became trivial enough to merge (each lost only its Linux
steps; Task 9 keeps the release workflow, its action pins and the verbatim Windows run), no task
was dropped, nothing was renumbered: 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5a, 5b, 6, 7a, 7b, P, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12 map to
themselves. The session cuts stay: 5a-1 = Steps 1–7, 5a-2 = Steps 1 and 8; 8a = Steps 1–4, 8b =
Steps 1 and 5–11.

**Header, Global Constraints, Conventions:**
- Goal and Tech Stack: a Windows zip only (CPack ZIP); Clang 19 only for the lint and the sanity
  build.
- New Global Constraint "Platform scope (U-WIN)": portable code, no Linux test runs, the Linux
  sanity build rule (final verification task and tasks changing Linux-only code or build files;
  portability bugs fixed, toolchain or third-party failures recorded), Linux release artefacts
  v1.x, the Linux custom_ffmpeg pin kept for the clang prefix, existing CI Linux jobs left alone,
  the accepted no-TSan risk.
- Toolchains, Shells, GPU tests (NVIDIA only), oracle presets (`vs2026` only), Long runs and
  parallelism rewritten for Windows-only runs.
- Conventions: the per-task checks (U-WIN matrix: `vs2026`, `vs2026-asan` with GPU tests on
  NVIDIA, clang-format, clang-tidy, privacy lint); the WSL env line and the WSL full-matrix command
  removed; new **Lint configure** (WSL `cmake --preset ninja-clang` only, a build only when
  clang-tidy needs generated shader headers) and **Linux sanity build** (WSL ninja-clang configure
  + build, no tests) entries; the lint runs after the lint configure.

**Per task:**
- Task 0: Step 6 lists the Linux archive layout with `cmake -E tar tf` only (no WSL, no
  `readelf`/`ldd`); Step 8 drops the WSL line (TXZ, Linux wx paths, xvfb, DISPLAY); the record and
  Produces drop the Linux dependency facts, TXZ and xvfb; the [U4] text names the Windows zip only;
  notes updated.
- Task 1: Step 3 drops the WSL script tests; Step 6's WSL `bootstrap.sh` run is the task's Linux
  sanity build (bootstrap.sh and the Linux pin are Linux-only build files); Step 7 reads the clang
  prefix's files only; Step 9 runs `vs2026` and `vs2026-asan` only; the versions comment and
  Produces name the Windows `ffmpeg/` only; a U-WIN note. The Linux pin and the package's Linux

- Task 2: Step 4 adds the Linux sanity build of dvv-platform (`dynamic_library_posix.cpp`, the
  POSIX `isAbsolutePath`); counts say both presets.
- Task 3: Step 5 drops the Linux counts and adds the lint configure; Step 9 (G1) runs `vs2026`
  only.
- Task 4: Step 5 (Linux tests) becomes the Linux sanity build of dvv-components (Linux branches of

- Task 5a: Execution notes Windows-only (env, full matrix, lint configure); Step 7 (WSL quick run)

- Task 5b: Execution notes as 5a; Step 5 (WSL quick run) removed and its case-6 fallback moved into

- Task 6: Execution notes as 5a; Step 1 drops the WSL program check; Step 6 (WSL) removed, Steps

## U-PROC changes

User decisions U-PROC and U-PROC-tidy (decisions.md, 2026-10-23).
- **Header and Global Constraints:** the Dispatch line names the Per-task and Checkpoint checks; the Linux sanity build is the checkpoint build (no per-task Linux build); new "Per-task checks" (vs2026 Debug build + all tests with the GPU and FFmpeg env, the Debug race check ×10 in the risky Task P, clang-format on the changed files, privacy lint), "Checkpoints" (Tasks 3, 5b, 7b, 9, 11; P under 9; Task 12 is gated publication without code and has no checkpoint) and "Checkpoint checks" (vs2026 Release, vs2026-asan with the ASan ×10 race checks since the last checkpoint, clang-tidy on the files changed since the last checkpoint after one WSL `ninja-clang` configure + build that is also the Linux sanity build; the dvv-components pathspecs). Toolchains, Shells, FFmpeg tests, Long runs and Task order follow.
- **Conventions:** the "Per-task checks (U-WIN matrix)", "Full matrix", "Lint configure", "Linux sanity build" and "Conventions lint" entries are replaced by "Per-task checks" and "Checkpoint checks". The part 2 "Execution notes" (Tasks 5a, 5b, 6, 7a, 7b) get the same change.
- **Task 1:** the WSL `bootstrap.sh` run stays as the clang-prefix install (it is not a Linux build of our code); the workspace tests run in `vs2026` only (ASan at the Task 3 checkpoint).
- **Tasks 2, 4, 7a, 8:** their per-task Linux sanity builds are removed; the Linux-only code is compiled by the next checkpoint build (Tasks 3, 5b, 7b, 9).
- **Every task:** the full-matrix and lint steps become Windows Debug and clang-format on the changed files. Release builds stay only where they are the task's subject (Task 8 packaging, Task 9 release-job rehearsal, Tasks 10 and P version check and measurement, build only); Tasks 7b's selftests and manual look use Debug.
- **Checkpoint tasks 3, 5b, 7b, 9:** a new last step "Checkpoint checks" with the full command lines; Tasks 3 and 7b use the workspace workflows (G1, G2) as the Windows Release and ASan runs.
- **Task 11 (final checkpoint):** Debug (Task 10's reused for unchanged heads), Release and ASan for every code repository, the Task P race check, the full clang-format and clang-tidy of every C++ repository after its `ninja-clang` build, the workspace Linux sanity build, the G3 workspace workflows and the privacy lint; the arbiter's reuse of Task 10's results is narrowed to Debug.
- **Task P:** the ×10 under `vs2026-asan` becomes a Debug ×10 in the task and an ASan ×10 at the Task 9 checkpoint.
- **Task 12:** the decline path's `bootstrap.sh` run is a clang-prefix refresh, not a sanity build.
- **Review fixes (uwin-uproc-review.md, controller decision):**

  - Part 2 Execution notes (Tasks 5a, 5b, 6, 7a, 7b): the env block is labelled "**Windows test env** (`<Windows test env>`)" to match the placeholder used in the commands.

## U-FF changes

Source: user decision U-FF (decisions.md, 2026-10-25, binding). The `ffmpeg` provider accepts any
container FFmpeg can demux (AVI, MKV, MOV, MP4, NUT, …). It decodes only lossless video codecs,
through an allowlist checked at open: `rawvideo`, `v210`, `v410`, `r210`, `ffv1`, `huffyuv`,
`ffvhuff`, `utvideo`, `magicyuv`, `png`, `qtrle`. Any other codec gets `Unsupported` "codec <name>
is not supported in v1.0 (lossless codecs only)". MPEG-family and other lossy codecs are out of
scope for M4/v1.0. Applied before execution. No task was added, dropped or renumbered; no STOP
step changed.

- **Header and Global Constraints:**
  - Goal and Architecture name the lossless scope.
  - The Design paragraph says U-FF overrides the design's `.m2v` fixtures and makes the U4 patent
    note and the BSD-3-Clause-Clear allowance depend on Task 0.
  - New Global Constraint "Codec scope (U-FF)": containers, the allowlist and its error text, lossy
    out of scope, and the runtime allowlist in every case. Its consequences: no patent-encumbered
    decoders in the bundled build → no U4 patent note; no SVT-AV1 in any pinned archive → no
    BSD-3-Clause-Clear allowance.
  - "Licence gate and bundling": each allowance only while a pinned archive needs it.
- **Task 0:**
  - Goal, Produces, the record block, the commit body and the Notes name the codec scope and the
    lossless fixture facts instead of the `.m2v` facts.
  - New Behaviour "Codec scope". A lossless-only build is pinned only if an attested LGPL
    custom_ffmpeg asset with no patent-encumbered decoder passes Steps 4–5; otherwise "none
    available" (M4 builds no variant). It records three consequences: U4 patent note
    kept/dropped; BSD-3-Clause-Clear allowance kept/dropped; LAME present/absent, with no LAME pin
    when absent.
  - Step 5: the fixture components are the lossless ones (formats `avi`, `mov`, `mp4`, `nut`;
    encoders `huffyuv`, `utvideo`, `magicyuv`, `png`; filter `setpts`); `mpegvideo`/`mpeg2video`
    are gone.
  - Step 6 records SVT-AV1 in the Linux archive.
  - Step 7 is new: "Codec scope and lossless fixture facts".
    - Part A: patent-encumbered decoders (H.264, HEVC, AAC, AV1, MPEG-1/2/4), the allowlist
      decoders, SVT-AV1, LAME, the `mpeg4` encoder and the lossless-only asset search.
    - Part B: the ffmpeg CLI commands for `seq.avi`/`seq.nut` (rawvideo), `seq.mkv` (FFV1 with
      `setpts=floor(N/2)/(25*TB)`, pairs of equal `pts`), the four `lossless_*` files and
      `lossy_mpeg4.mp4`.
    - Choice of the `Sequential` fixture: the first of AVI and NUT with a `pts` of N/A, else
      `seq.mkv` with equal `pts`, else stop.
  - Step 8 skips the LAME tarball with "LAME: absent".
  - Step 10's [U4] text says rawreel opens only lossless codecs and loses the patent sentences when
    they are "dropped".
- **Task 1:**
  - Goal and Consumes name the codec-scope record.
  - New Behaviour "Codec scope". With "BSD-3-Clause-Clear allowance: dropped": no BSD-3-Clause-Clear
    in the gate, the SPDX expressions, the tests (expect 0) or the comments, and the first commit
    is retitled. With "LAME: absent": no LGPL-2.0-or-later and no LAME pin. With a lossless-only
    build pinned: its asset, SHA256, licence files and SPDX.
  - A U-FF note.
- **Task 5a:**
  - Goal and commit body: any container, lossless only.
  - `ffmpeg_rules.h` gains `LOSSLESS_CODECS` and `checkLosslessCodec`. Open step 3b checks the
    allowlist on `avcodec_get_name(codecpar->codec_id)` before the index scan and the decoder
    lookup.
  - New Behaviour "Containers (U-FF)": AVI `pts` from the index-based `dts`, keyframes and seeks
    through the AVI index, the same rules for every container.
  - Unit tests: `match confidence` uses neutral demuxer inputs (`image2`, `avi`) instead of
    `mpegvideo`/`m2v`; new case 8 `lossless codec allowlist`.
  - Provider cases:
    - Case 2: the lossless `<seq>` from Task 0.
    - Cases 5 and 12: `long.<ext>` (3000 FFV1 frames, same `Sequential` trigger, at least 2 s to
      decode, else 704×576).
    - Case 6: PNG `gray` concat parts, message "frame 5 changed to 176x144 gray".
    - Case 9: AVI and MOV heads, 90 with and without the extension.
    - New case 13: huffyuv AVI, utvideo MKV, v210 MOV and magicyuv NUT match `framemd5`, with seeks.
    - New case 14: refusal. A copy of the AVI with the `HFYU` FourCC patched to `XVID` (codec
      `mpeg4`) always; `lossy_mpeg4.mp4` when the `mpeg4` encoder exists.
  - Fixture commands replace both `.m2v` files and the MPEG-2 concat parts.
  - Counts: unit 8 → 9 cases; ffmpeg 17 → 19 cases (the 5a-1 cut, Steps 5–6, Expected results).
  - Notes: U-FF notes; the concat fallback is `.nut` instead of `.ts`.
- **Task 5b:** counts 25 → 27 (Steps 3–4, Expected), 17 → 19 for the wrong-suite hint, the drop
  fallback 24 → 26. No fixture change (lavfi only).
- **Task 6:** no change except a U-FF note. The oracle uses only rawvideo and v210 (both on the
  allowlist).
- **Task 7a:**
  - Step 1 reads Task 0's U-FF records.
  - The 64×48 `seq.m2v` becomes the 64×48 `<seq>`, and e2e case 5 also checks `access
    sequential`.
  - New e2e case 8 (only with an `mpeg4` encoder): `info lossy.mp4` exits 3 with the allowlist
    message.
  - Commit body updated.
- **Task 10:**
  - Goal and Consumes name U-FF.
  - Spec: §2 FFmpeg row (lossless only); §7.1/§7.2 the allowlist, the error text, `Sequential` for
    missing or equal `pts`, and a rewrite of any sentence naming MPEG-2/H.264 as supported; §13.2
    the gate additions as Task 1 made them; §17 a U-FF line and the BSD-3-Clause-Clear line
    conditional.
  - README:
    - dvv-components names the allowlist.
    - rawreel "FFmpeg sources" names the containers, the allowlist and the refusal text.
    - "Limits of v1.0" says only lossless codecs open.
    - The [U4] accept text adds "rawreel itself opens only lossless video codecs". Its patent
      sentences go with "dropped", and LAME goes with "absent".
    - The decline text says the allowlist applies to any build.
  - Release notes describe the scope.
  - Step 4 adds a U-FF stale-text check (H.264, HEVC, MPEG-1/2/4, `.m2v`, lossy, elementary stream).
  - A U-FF note.
- **Task 11:**
  - E5 item 12 becomes "codec scope": (a) `clip.avi` (huffyuv) plays; (b) a lossy file (the
    user's H.264 MP4, else `clip-lossy.mp4`, MPEG-4) is refused with the allowlist message.
  - The NVENC `clip-h264.mp4` is gone.
  - Steps 5, 6 and 8 follow.
  - E1 names the lossless and refusal cases and 27 doctest cases (28 after the review fixes below).
  - The arbiter note on item 12 is replaced by a U-FF note.
- **Task 12:**
  - Every STOP step and the Gate B question are unchanged.
  - Inside the Gate A [U4] bracket: "although rawreel opens only lossless codecs", plus the
    U-FF variant without the patent parenthesis ("dropped") and without LAME ("absent").
  - Pre-flight Step 4 names the open item "12 (b) open: no lossy file".
- **Task P, 2–4, 7b, 8, 9:** no change. Task 8's sources archive and Task 9 drop LAME only with
  "LAME: absent", and the controller names those places in the dispatch (Task 0 Behaviour).
- **Superseded records:** the "Arbiter fixes applied (plan v2)" table is kept as history. Its rows
  1 (counts 5 → 17 → 25; now 5 → 19 → 28), 21 (H.264 item), 26 (17 cases), 29 (the `.m2v` probe
  score) and 36 (the user checkpoint; U-GO-M4) are superseded by the texts above and below.

  - Task 5b `GraphSupplier::build` parses with `avfilter_graph_segment_parse`, checks every filter
    name with the new rule `checkLavfiFilter` (`LAVFI_FILE_FILTERS`, 44 FFmpeg 8 names: `movie`,
    `amovie`, `subtitles`, `sendcmd`, `zmq`, `metadata`, `drawtext`, …) before any filter is
    created, then calls `avfilter_graph_segment_apply` (the same result as
    `avfilter_graph_parse_ptr`).
  - Errors: `movie`/`amovie` give exactly "lavfi: movie/amovie are not supported; open the file
    directly". Other names give "lavfi: filter <name> opens files or URLs and is not supported; open
    the file directly". This is a plan choice: the controller's single text names only movie/amovie.
  - Step 1 checks the list against `ffmpeg -h full` of the pinned build.
  - New tests: ffmpeg case 9 `lavfi: graphs that open files or URLs are refused`, where `movie=` on
    Task 5a's huffyuv AVI would play without the check; unit case `lavfi file filters`.
  - Counts: ffmpeg 27 → 28, the drop fallback 26 → 27, Task 11 E1 28.
  - Text updates: open step 6, the commit body, Notes, the Global Constraint "Codec scope" (a new
    "No bypass through `lavfi:`" bullet), the spec §2 row, the README trust note (now "file rule"),
    and a Task 0 Step 10 / Task 10 note.
  - The [U4] sentence "rawreel itself opens only lossless video codecs" is kept and is now true.
- **Minor items applied:**
  - (a) LAME split into "LAME allowance" (gate: any pinned archive, Linux included) and "LAME:
    present/absent" (shipped build: source pin, sources archive, texts). Changed: Task 0 Produces,
    Behaviour, Step 6 (Linux LAME fact), Step 7 and Step 11; Task 1 Consumes and Behaviour; the
    Architecture bullet (each allowance and the LAME pin conditional); Task 8 smoke step 10 (LAME
    entry only with "LAME: present").
  - (b) A lossless-only candidate qualifies only with no video decoder outside the allowlist. The
    patent note is dropped only then. Part A prints "video decoders outside the allowlist".
  - (c) Task 5a case 13 now says frame 7 is in the ring; it seeks for 1 and then reads 0 from the
    ring.
  - (d) Task 0 Step 7 Part B writes and probes the FourCC-patched `lossy_xvid.avi` (codec `mpeg4`,
    opens) and records it. Task 11 item 12 (b) falls back to a patched `clip-lossy.avi` without an
    `mpeg4` encoder, so it no longer stays open for lack of a lossy file.
  - (e) New pure rule `sequentialMode` with unit case 9 `sequential mode`, which covers the NOPTS
    trigger that the lossless fixtures may never produce. Task 5a unit 9 → 10 cases. A note says
    `seq.nut` can never qualify and is kept only as the user's example.
  - (f) U-IO2 interplay: the ffmpeg and lavfi providers ignore `fileReads` and set `reader` to
    `"ffmpeg"` / `"lavfi"`. Task 7a Consumes lists M3.1's `core/config`, `--config` and
    `SessionOptions::fileReads`, which the startup refactor keeps.
  - (g) Note: MOV and MP4 share the `mov` demuxer, so the MOV fixtures cover MP4.
  - (h) Arbiter rows 1 and 36 were added to the superseded list.
- **Not applied:** an always-on CLI refusal in Task 7a, because CMake cannot write the patched
  bytes simply. Task 5a case 14 (a) covers the provider.

**Open points for the controller:**
- Lossless codecs never reorder frames, so libavformat normally fills a missing `pts` from `dts`,
  AVI included. The user's examples (rawvideo in AVI or NUT) will then likely not lack timestamps.
  In that case the `Sequential` fixture uses D5's equal-`pts` trigger (`seq.mkv`). Task 0 Step 7
  decides by measurement and stops if neither works.
- A lossless-only custom_ffmpeg build exists only if custom_ffmpeg publishes one. M4 does not make
  one. With the full v1.0.0 build the U4 patent note and the BSD-3-Clause-Clear allowance stay,
  and the runtime allowlist applies.

## U-M4ORDER changes

User decision U-M4ORDER (2026-10-25, binding; `decisions.md`): Task 8 (packaging) moves after Task
10, and Task 10 (release preparation) is split into 10-alpha, 10-beta and 10-final. New order:
0 → 1 → 2 → 3 → 4 → 5a → 5b → 6 → 7a → 7b → P → 9 → 10-alpha → 10-beta → 10-final → 8 → 11 → 12.
It supersedes the order of arbiter row 7 and of the earlier change sections. Applied:
- **Header:** a "Task order" paragraph (the order, the three stages and what each holds, the
  meaning of "Task 10" in older texts); the task texts now stand in execution order (Task 9,
  Task 10 with its stages, Task 8, Task 11, Task 12; Task P at the end).
- **Global Constraints:** codec-scope references (Task 10-beta); the Git line (version in
  10-alpha); Checkpoints (the Task 9 checkpoint now covers Task P and Task 9; Task 8's packaging
  work, which it covered before, moves to the Task 11 final checkpoint, which runs right after
  Task 8; the three stages fall under Task 11 as Task 10 did); Task order with the dependencies;
  workflow group G3 (P, 9, 10-alpha, 10-beta, 10-final, 8, 11). Conventions: package tests also in
  the three stages.
- **Task 9:** Goal and a "Place in the order" paragraph; Consumes names Task 8's planned interface
  (the scripts do not exist yet) and 10-beta/10-final for the release notes; Produces says Task 8
  first runs the workflow's commands. Plan v2's Steps 4 (the Windows job's commands verbatim) and
  5 (the checksum lines) moved to Task 8 Steps 9b and 9c; the new Step 4 checks every script call
  and output name of `release.yml` against Task 8's Produces; Steps 6–9 became 5–8. The Step 8
  checkpoint covers Task P's repository when P ran, otherwise it records "no build input changed"
  (workflow files only, checked by actionlint, the static checks and Step 4). Expected results,
  Notes (pre-release versions are never packaged; the `check` job's version rule stays).
- **Task 10:** replaced by an overview (item-to-stage table, the version label mechanism) and
  three self-contained stages. Every former Task 10 item is in exactly one stage:
  - 10-alpha: the version inventory and edits (`project(VERSION 1.0.0)`, the `1.0` requests,
    `<project>_VERSION_STRING` = `${PROJECT_VERSION}-alpha`, `rawreel_dvv_version()` and
    `RAWREEL_VERSION` read it); spec §2 FFmpeg row, §7.1/§7.2, §7.3 and the `rawreel.json` text
    (U-IO2-config); rawreel README items 2 and 7; new item 8 "Running a development build" and
    the run instructions for the user; not a review stop.
  - 10-beta: label `-beta`; fixes for alpha feedback; the other spec items (§1.1/§15, §3.1, §4,
    §6, §9.1, §9.3, §10.2, §10.3, §11, §12, §13.2, §13.4, §13.5, §15, §16, §17); the workspace,
    dvv-components, dvv-platform and dvv-media READMEs; rawreel README items 1, 3–6 and the
    licence line; the [U4] accept and decline texts; the licence notes (§13.2, §17's
    BSD-3-Clause-Clear line, the release notes' licence line); the release-notes draft; the
    stale-text check.
  - 10-final: label removed (version 1.0.0); the final release notes; a label sweep; the ledger
    with the heads that Task 11 reuses.
  - Version strings follow semver (`1.0.0-alpha`, `1.0.0-beta`, `1.0.0`). CMake's
    `project(VERSION)` takes numbers only, so the label lives in `<project>_VERSION_STRING`, which
    the package configurations export; package version files and the `1.0` requests stay numeric.
  - The check of the docs' package commands against the dry-run outputs (former Task 10 Step 5)
    moved to Task 8 Step 9d, because the packages exist only after Task 8.
  - Each stage runs the full `ctest --preset vs2026` (package tests included), clang-format and the
    privacy lint on all eight repositories, as former Task 10 Step 7 did.
- **Task 8:** a "Place in the order" paragraph; Step 1 checks that the version is 1.0.0 with no
  label (it stops otherwise); Consumes adds Task 9's `release.yml` and the three stages' outputs;
  the Linux sanity build of `packaging.cmake` and the Release/ASan/clang-tidy checks are the Task
  11 checkpoint's (was Task 9's); new Steps 9b (the `release.yml` Windows-job commands verbatim,
  with the test list), 9c (the checksum lines) and 9d (the docs' package commands); Files,
  Expected results, Commit and Notes follow. The 8a/8b cut is unchanged (9b–9d are in 8b).
- **Task 11:** references only: Consumes (Tasks 0–9, the three stages and 8); the Debug reuse
  now names 10-final Steps 7 and 8 (rawreel and rawreel-workspace change in Task 8, so they run in
  full). Nothing else changed.
- **Task 12:** references only: the [U4] decline text is 10-beta's (Files, Consumes, decline
  path). Every STOP step is unchanged.
- **Task P:** it runs before Task 9 (was Task 8), and a remaining miss does not block the later
  tasks.

Task 5a (recorded at the Task 5b checkpoint, from the Task 5a verification):
- The `ffmpeg` provider matches files by FFmpeg's content probe only (`matchConfidence`: 90 when
  the probe score is above 50, the extension-only score; raw and Y4M demuxers stay excluded).
- `codec_whitelist` is set to the lossless allowlist (U-FF) before `avformat_find_stream_info`
  probes the streams, so no lossy decoder is opened even while probing.
- The frame index comes from the container when it lists every frame (AVI `idx1`, MOV sample
  tables); otherwise a demux-only packet scan builds it at open, and the scan checks the stop
  flag before each packet.
- Known v1.0 limit: MKV and NUT have no complete frame index (cues and syncpoints list only some
  frames), so those files are scanned at open. The README and the release notes list it as a
  known limit (Task 10).

Task 5b (2026-10-25):
- Security review (controller decision): the lavfi filter denylist (`LAVFI_FILE_FILTERS`, 44
  names in the plan, 51 after the build check) is replaced by an allowlist,
  `LAVFI_ALLOWED_FILTERS` in `src/sources/ffmpeg_rules.h`; a denylist cannot be complete (it
  missed qrencode, qrencodesrc, flite, nnedi, asr and filters with font, text, weights, model,
  stats or dump file options). Every filter name of a graph must be on the list, checked on the
  parsed names before any filter is created; any other name is `Unsupported` "lavfi: filter
  <name> is not supported in v1.0" (this replaces both "movie/amovie" and "opens files or URLs"
  messages). The list (34 names, all present in the pinned n8.1.3 build, none with an option
  that takes a path or URL): generators testsrc, testsrc2, smptebars, smptehdbars, pal75bars,
  pal100bars, rgbtestsrc, yuvtestsrc, colorchart, colorspectrum, color, gradients, allrgb,
  allyuv, nullsrc, mandelbrot; processing format, scale, setparams, setsar, setdar, fps, trim,
  select, crop, pad, hflip, vflip, transpose, null, loop, tpad, drawbox, drawgrid. Dropped from
  the proposed list: cellauto (its `filename` option reads a pattern file). drawtext is not
  allowed (font files). This supersedes the "44 file filters" wording of U-FF, Global
  Constraints and Task 5b.
- Consequences in the tests: `lavfi:nosuchfilter` and `lavfi:sine` are now refused by the
  allowlist (`Unsupported`); the "graph is invalid" case uses `lavfi:testsrc2=nosuchoption=1`.
  The sink's "not a video output" error cannot be reached through an allowed graph (all allowed
  filters are video); the check stays as a defence. New case "lavfi: every allowed generator
  opens" (each generator with `,format=yuv420p`, frames 0 and 1). The unit case is "lavfi filter
  allowlist". The six reference graphs use only testsrc2 and format and are unchanged.
- `lavfi:` is an opaque URI scheme: `parseUri` keeps the graph in `Uri::body` and gives no query.
  A `?` in the body is refused ("lavfi: URIs take no query (the rest of the URI is the graph)").
- The pinned FFmpeg evaluates scale's `n` one ahead of the output frame number, so the
  size-change case uses `lt(n,4)` to change the size at frame 3.
- A failed pull (a size or format change, a sink error) makes the next request rebuild the graph,
  so the frame numbering never shifts.
- Suite `ffmpeg` had 25 cases before Task 5b (5 loader + 20 provider), so it has 35 after it.
- Checkpoint (Tasks 4, 5a, 5b; dvv-components): clang-tidy found findings in Task 4 and 5a code
  as well (unchecked optional access, a static constant's case, `from_chars` on `data()`,
  vector growth in loops, int multiplications widened to `size_t`); fixed in one `fix:` commit.

Task 5b review fix (2026-10-25):
- One `av_buffersink_get_frame` call runs the graph until a frame comes out, and nothing can stop
  it. Over an unbounded generator a filter that drops frames can make one pull endless
  (`testsrc2,select=0` hung the open; `trim=start_frame=<huge>` and `fps=<tiny>` discard without
  bound). So `select`, `trim` and `fps` are removed from `LAVFI_ALLOWED_FILTERS` (31 names now).
  Every remaining processing filter gives one output frame per input frame (`loop` and `tpad`
  only add frames), so one pull costs one generated frame and close waits at most for that.
- A graph is one chain: after apply, a filter with more than one input is `Unsupported` "lavfi:
  filter <name> with <n> inputs is not supported in v1.0 (a graph is one chain)". scale's
  optional `ref` input (`rw`/`rh`) joins two generators by time and discarded 25 million frames
  per output frame in the test graph.
- The refusal text is now "lavfi: filter <name> is not supported in v1.0; open files with file:
  instead" (the U-FF hint is back; the start is unchanged).
- `SourceInfo::deterministic` is false when the graph text (lower-cased, without `'` and `\`)
  contains `gradients` (random seed and colours by default), `random` or `bikeshed` (random
  colours of any colour option): `lavfiDeterministic` in `ffmpeg_rules`.
- The unreachable "not a video output" branch is removed; a sink link error is `ffmpegError`'s.
- Tests: refusal of select, trim and fps graphs and of a two-input scale; bypass forms (a second
  chain, `movie@x`, `'mov''ie'`, `mov\ie`) refused under the parsed name; `lavfi:null`
  (unconnected input) and two outputs; random graphs not deterministic; the lavfi stop case is
  paced (the `PacedWorkers` seam moved into the ffmpeg test harness).
- For Task 10-alpha: the spec §2 sentence, README item 2 and the Interfaces line cite the
  allowlist (`LAVFI_ALLOWED_FILTERS`, generators plus basic one-in-one-out video filters, one
  chain) and the one refusal text above, not `LAVFI_FILE_FILTERS` or the movie/amovie and "opens
  files or URLs" messages. Known v1.0 limit for the README: a lavfi graph is one chain without
  frame-dropping filters.

Task 7a review fix (2026-10-25):
- Brief defect, step 6: `bench` always sets up the GPU, so the `bench "<lavfi>" --frames 30` step
  moved from `run-cli-ffmpeg.cmake` (label `ffmpeg`) to `run-cli-ffmpeg-gpu.cmake` (labels
  `gpu;ffmpeg`, the M2 77 rule), after the 77 probe and with `expect_no_validation`. The `ffmpeg`
  e2e test needs no GPU now (a GPU-less runner with FFmpeg skips instead of failing).
- Brief defect, `--json` open errors: the JSON `message` is `error.message` plus the FFmpeg status
  suffix (`openErrorMessage`), as every other `--json` error; stderr keeps `openErrorText`
  (`describe()` plus the suffix). `run-cli-ffmpeg-missing.cmake` checks the JSON message.
- Crash handler (beyond the brief's binding code): the state is trivially destructible at
  namespace scope (fixed path buffers, never destroyed, so a crash at exit still finds it); the
  dump is written by a dump thread created at install time and woken by the filter (the filter
  uses almost no stack, so a stack overflow is dumped); the first crashing thread wins, later
  ones wait (`Sleep(INFINITE)`) instead of returning `EXCEPTION_CONTINUE_SEARCH` to WER; the
  crashing thread waits at most 120 s for the dump. `install` returns the "crash dumps are off"
  warning, which the CLI and the GUI log once their sinks exist. `RAWREEL_TEST_CRASH` also takes
  `stack`, `threads` and `exit`; `rawreel.cli_e2e.crash` runs all four modes and checks the
  exception exit code (Access violation / Stack overflow) and exactly one dump line.

Task 8: packaging (2026-10-25). Records and deviations of the packaging task:
- Inputs: the notices format of `write-notices.cmake` (a separator of at least 20 `=`, then
  `<name> <version>`, `Licence: …`, licence blocks `--- <path> ---`, and the paragraph
  `Microsoft Visual C++ runtime`); `RAWREEL_MIN_VCRT_VERSION` = 14.44; `OWN_PREFIX` = OFF (the
  developer prefix is bundled, so the not-bundled run builds its own prefix with

  clone's and each submodule's `.git` (the brief's recursive glob deleted sources), fails on an

  repository privacy lint).

- Performance (N4, M4-L-XCONN): sampling the GUI thread during maximised playback showed it
  waiting for GDK's shared X connection (`_XSend` -> `xcb_take_socket`) because the presenter's
  Vulkan surface used it. The surface now uses a private X connection opened on the GDK
  display's name (the plan's recorded fallback). Perf selftest, three runs each, before -> after:
  GUI thread in that wait 44-52 % -> 0.3 %; event-loop busy runs of 50 ms or more 99-122 -> 2-3
  per run (at start-up); frames presented per 600-frame run 148-256 -> 281-306; wall time
  27.8-30.1 s -> 26.9-27.3 s. `actualFps`, stalls and lateness did not change (CPU contention of
  the CPU renderer). It also removed the WSI errors on GDK's connection.
- Merge (M4-L-FIXDEC, U-SQ2): rawreel's 20 `m4l` commits (`b08f07d..256dcfd`) became the one
  commit `4987d55` on `main` with the same tree; its body lists the old commits. The dvv-*
  repositories had no M4-L change.
- NOT VALIDATED until the Gate A CI run: the release workflow on GitHub runners (setup-python
  and pip, the apt lines and the GTK package name in the container, the job permissions and the
  attestation check on Linux, bootstrap from scratch, `xvfb-run` on the runner, the artifact
  uploads and their merge, the Windows upload change, actionlint); the CI tar owner and group
  rule (owners `root` or `runner`, groups `root`, `runner` or `docker`; locally only printed);
  the clean-container check (no local Docker). Also not checked: a native Linux desktop and a
  real GPU on Linux (only WSLg, the CPU renderer and Xvfb), R1 on a native window manager, and
  HiDPI (scales other than 1x).
- v1.x backlog items it created: "Paused repaint count", "Gtk-CRITICAL GtkScrollbar lines",
  "Dark appearance on Linux/GTK", and "LF review M5/M6" (a test that catches the presenter going
  back to GDK's shared X connection; the GTK fixed-size format dialog flickering with a window
  manager that resizes it during a drag). Native Wayland, the Linux file-I/O default review, the
  Linux frame-timer jitter check, a Linux crash handler and the AppImage stay v1.x as before.
